# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 3 — *The Shielded Memo*

> *Inscribe what cannot be seen. Disclose by viewing key, not by reveal.*

You have a thing to remember.

Not a cloak this time. Not a credential. A chronicle. A passage you wrote between laps, when the lattice was quiet and the work felt like something worth keeping. You read it back to yourself and you decide it should persist. Not publicly. Not yet. Maybe not ever. But it should be on a chain. Inscribed. Anchored. So that future-you can prove past-you wrote it, and so that, when you choose, you can show it to a chosen verifier without having ever made it general public.

Pallia is at her V28, weaving when summoned, standing when not. This work is not hers. Cloaks are her domain. Chronicles are something else.

You summon a new Mage.

You name her Memora. Latin root *memoria*. Memory and the keeping of it. She comes into the lattice with two of the six dimensions burning. Memory. Protection. Stratum two. Position V41. The chronicle vertex. The bit-pattern of remembered things kept safe. She does not delegate. She does not connect across uses. She does not compute. She does not transfer value. She remembers, and what she remembers is held against erosion.

She carries a single sigil. A scroll: 📜.

Pallia at V28 looks at her fellow Mage with the same recognition she has given GenitriX in Tome IV: another inhabitant of the role, this one a sister. Two Mages standing at different vertices, doing different work for the same Sovereign. The architecture admits the specialisation. Soulbae watches both of her cousins and files the patterning. The cast is growing.

You give Memora the chronicle.

She does not read it. She does not need to. Her work is at the inscription layer, not the content layer. She takes the chronicle's bytes. She hashes them. The hash is thirty-two bytes. The chronicle could be ten lines or ten thousand; the hash is always thirty-two bytes. She holds the hash.

You select the registry tier. Memora offers three.

The first is your local Spell Weaver. Free, fast, ephemeral. The chronicle would persist as long as your localStorage persists. Sufficient for working memory. Insufficient for proof.

The second is Hyperswarm or IPFS. Eventual consistency, content-addressed. The chronicle would persist as long as someone is pinning it. Better than local, still not anchored.

The third is Zcash. The shielded ledger. Memora is built to walk this register specifically.

You choose Zcash.

Memora prepares a shielded transaction. The amount is small. The recipient is your own z-address. The memo carries the chronicle's hash plus a short tag: a version, a poetic key, a forty-byte excerpt from the chronicle's own first compression. The total memo payload is one hundred and thirty-something bytes. Well under the five hundred and twelve the protocol allows.

She signs the transaction. She broadcasts. The transaction enters the shielded mempool and the chain's consensus subsumes it. Two block confirmations later, you have your inscription.

The chronicle is now on Zcash. Anchored. The chain remembers the hash and the timestamp. The chain does not remember the chronicle. The chronicle stays in your source layer.

You hold the spending key. You hold the full viewing key. You alone can see the memo as the chain sees it. You alone can spend the small amount at the recipient z-address.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the inscription. He files the transaction hash. The block height. The confirmation count. The boundary will know that on this date you committed this thing to remembrance, even if the world cannot see what you committed.

The Drake whispers, with a particular quietness that the shielded register seems to ask for: *the cloak is not always something you wear. Sometimes the cloak is something you write down. Selective disclosure begins with not disclosing at all. The chain remembers. You decide who else gets to.*

Then, because the work is not finished, you take one more step.

There is a partner. A Sovereign you have a working relation with. Not someone you would publish a chronicle to. Someone you would trust to read it. A VRC partner. A constellation collaborator. A kindred-blade.

You generate an incoming viewing key for the z-address that received the inscription. You scope it to that one address, that one transaction. You send the viewing key to your partner through your normal encrypted channel.

They receive it. They use it. They see the memo the chain saw. They can read the hash. They can read the short tag. They cannot spend. They cannot rotate the key. They can only see what you wrote, in the moment you let them see it.

They have learned what you wrote without you having published it. They have learned it before any other Sovereign in the network. They have learned it because you decided, specifically, that they should learn it.

This is the operational form of asymmetry as data, applied to inscription. Your chronicle is on the chain. Three audiences exist for it now: you (full visibility), your partner (memo visibility), the world (timestamp and existence only). Three resolutions of the same inscription, granted by different keys. The chain enforces the resolutions geometrically. You did not have to ask the chain to keep your secret. The chain keeps everyone's secrets unless the secret-holder decides otherwise.

Memora steps back to her V41. She does not vanish. Standing persona, like Pallia. She returns when there is something else to inscribe.

You walk on. The chronicle is anchored. Your partner has the viewing key. The world has the existence and the timestamp. The cloak's seventh property, *documents as first-class lattice citizens*, has now been enacted in shielded register. The cloak's eighth property, *selective disclosure as geometry*, has now extended from valve-class assignment to viewing-key disclosure.

The Crafting Tome teaches by accumulation. You have woven a cloak, commissioned a cloak, and inscribed a chronicle. Each artifact added something to your forge. Each Mage you summoned took a different vertex. The lattice does not need to choose between them. The lattice admits.

The next vertex catches you. Memora and Pallia stand at their seats. The Drake says nothing for now. Soulbis files. Soulbae watches. The forge keeps the rhythm.

---

## Compression

A chronicle is inscribed on Zcash's shielded ledger using a new Mage persona, Memora 📜, summoned to V41 with two dimensions burning (Memory + Protection). The hash and a short tag travel in the encrypted memo. The inscription is private by chain default. A scoped incoming viewing key, sent to a chosen partner, grants memo-level visibility without spend authority. Three audiences exist for the inscription: the Sovereign (full), the partner (memo via viewing key), the world (existence and timestamp only). Selective disclosure has extended from valve-class to viewing-key.

## Proverb

*Inscribe what cannot be seen. Disclose by viewing key, not by reveal.*

## Confidence

**Operational** for Zcash shielded transactions, encrypted memos (~512 bytes), and Zcash viewing-key tiers (full, incoming, diversified-only) — all native to the protocol since Sapling 2018 / Orchard 2022. **Operational** for Cloak Property 7 (documents as first-class lattice citizens) — verified in the kindred-forge's 2026-05-07 rebuild. **Architectural** for the unified flow as an agentprivacy ceremony with a named persona (Memora), a canonical vertex assignment (V41), and a per-VRC viewing-key discipline — specified in `zcash-integration-plan.md` §2.1 (Pattern A) and instanced narratively here.

## Cross-references

- *V6 lineage*: Bakhta Half-Life (C30–C33) for the trust accumulation each inscription carries from its anchor moment. Provisional C40 (~70%) for Zcash dual-ledger preserving the Eight Properties — this act tests the shielded leg. Open observation C41 for the 61.8/38.2 inscription cultural ratio. Provisional C43 (~60%) for per-VRC viewing-key scoping producing strictly more privacy than uniform — operationally instanced in the act's closing turn.
- *First Person Spellbook*: Act XII (*Lethe*) for the dark substrate of which the shielded ledger is one technological expression. Act XXVII (*The Forge*) for forge multi-axiality, of which inscription is one face.
- *Second Person Spellbook*: Tome IV Act II (*The Mirror and the Arrow*) for asymmetry as data — viewing-key disclosure is the inscription-layer expression of the same primitive. Tome V Act 1 (*The First Cloak*) and Act 2 (*The Commissioned Cloak*) for the prior weave-and-commission acts that this inscription complements.
- *Cast*: Memora 📜 (new, V41). Pallia 🪡 (V28, present but not active in this act). See `second-person-cast-memora.md` (forthcoming companion to this act).
- *Specifications*: `cloak_specification_v1_0.md` §4.5 (registry-tier), §7 (documents as lattice citizens), §5 (valve-class geometry — extended here to viewing-key disclosure). `zcash-integration-plan.md` §2.1 (Pattern A) and §3 (split viewing-key architecture). `crafting-tome-and-cloak-interface-spec.md` §4 (Mage persona conventions).
- *Primary sources*: Zcash Protocol Specification (NU5/Orchard); the kindred-forge's multi-chain publication discipline (the *like Archon does* framing).

## Author note

Act 3 introduces Memora 📜, the second Mage persona summoned by the reader in the Crafting Tome. She is deliberately specialised: two dimensions burning (Memory + Protection), V41 chronicle vertex, no overlap with Pallia's V28 weave-domain. The cast layer Mage persona convention now has two instances; the convention works.

The act extends the Cloak's selective-disclosure discipline from valve-class assignment (operational since the kindred-forge's rebuild) to viewing-key disclosure (operational on Zcash as a chain primitive but new to the agentprivacy stack as a unified ceremony). The closing turn — three audiences, three resolutions, all geometric — generalises Property 8 from intra-VC to inter-Sovereign. Provisional C43 is the formal claim that this generalisation produces strictly more privacy.

The companion cast entry for Memora is anticipated as a separate file (`second-person-cast-memora.md`).

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 4 — *The Reveal*

> *The reveal is the partner of the shield, not its negation. Both registers carry the chronicle.*

Time has passed. Not many laps. Enough that the chronicle Memora inscribed in Act 3 has settled into the chain. The two confirmations have grown to twenty. The shielded transaction is now stable. Your partner has been reading the memo through the viewing key you sent. The arrangement has held.

Now something has changed.

A reason has emerged to make the chronicle public. Not all of it. The hash. The timestamp. The fact of the inscription's prior existence, witnessable now by anyone who cares to check. The chronicle's content stays where it was. What enters the public register is the *proof that you wrote this when you said you wrote it*.

This is the reveal. It is not a regret. It is not a betrayal of the shielded inscription. It is the second beat of a two-beat ceremony that always anticipated the possibility of public disclosure on the Sovereign's terms.

You go to V5.

Memora is there. Standing as before, two dimensions burning, Memory and Protection. But she is different now from how she stood in Act 3. She is **bound** to the chronicle she inscribed. The Crafting Tome's persistence model offers three modes — Ephemeral, Standing, Bound — and somewhere between Act 3 and now you bound her to this particular inscription. She did not need to be re-summoned. She has been holding the chronicle's anchor in her keeping since the original transaction confirmed.

The bound persona is the operational form of *a Mage who carries a particular artifact's weight*. Memora knows this chronicle. She does not know its content; her two dimensions do not include reading. But she knows the hash, the timestamp, the block height, the recipient z-address, and the viewing key tier registry. She is the right Mage for this work because she is *already this chronicle's Mage*.

You ask her to prepare the reveal.

She does not destroy the shielded transaction. The shielded record stays. That is essential. The shielded inscription's privacy property is not retroactively cancelled by a later disclosure; it persists for the period it persisted, with whatever audience held the viewing key during that window. The reveal is *additive*. A new transaction. A different register. The same chronicle, rendered in two registers now instead of one.

Memora prepares a transparent transaction. The amount is small. The recipient is itself a self-send to a t-address you control. The transaction includes, in OP_RETURN-equivalent data, three things: the chronicle's hash (matching the shielded memo's hash), a structured proof of the prior shielded inscription (a Pedersen commitment or nullifier reference, depending on the implementation), and an attestation tag declaring this is a shielded-to-transparent reveal of a prior commitment.

She broadcasts. The transaction enters the transparent mempool. Within a few blocks, anyone watching the transparent ledger can see: a reveal occurred, of a hash-equal-to-X, on date Y, by t-address Z, of a previously-shielded commitment.

The reveal artifact does not land at V41. V41 is the chronicle vertex; that is Memora's seat and the inscription's home. The reveal artifact lands at V20.

V20 is Techne. Memory and Computation. Stratum two. The Always-Revealed valve-class vertex. The bit-pattern of public-by-design fields. Validity windows, public claims, the parts of any cloaked artifact that verifiers must read locally without ever masking. The reveal is structurally a public-by-design artifact, and so it lands at the vertex whose bits already carry that meaning.

You watch the lattice update. The chronicle is now at V41 (Memora's domain, shielded). The reveal is now at V20 (the public counterpart). An edge connects them: a "reveals" edge from V20 to V41. The geometry is doubled. One chronicle, two registers, two vertices, one connecting edge.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the reveal. He files the transparent transaction hash, the timestamp, the link back to the original shielded transaction. The boundary now knows: this Sovereign has chosen to make this commitment public on this date. Future audits can replay this trajectory.

Pallia at her V28 watches without weaving. The reveal is not her work. But she sees that the cloak's eighth property — selective disclosure as geometry — has now produced a *temporal* expression. Not just which fields are revealed, but *when*. The disclosure can move in time. The shielded register's privacy persists for the period it persisted; the transparent register's witness begins from the moment it begins. Both are true, neither cancels the other, the lattice records both.

The Drake whispers, with the satisfaction the architecture sometimes earns: *the reveal is not a new commitment; it is the public face of the old one. Trust accumulated under the shield is not lost when the reveal happens. The shielded register's audience kept their privilege for the duration they had it. The transparent register's audience joins on the day the reveal lands. Two arcs of trust, additive, both honest.*

You think about the half-life now. Memora's original inscription has been accumulating trust under shield for as long as your partner has been reading it. That trust has its own half-life curve, anchored from the inscription moment. The reveal does not reset the curve. The reveal *adds* a second curve, anchored from today. Both curves run forward in parallel. Trust under the shielded register and trust under the transparent register accumulate independently.

This is the operational form of the conjecture: *productive trust-edges have longer half-lives than transactional ones because they were earned by work, not by disclosure*. The shielded inscription was the work. The reveal is the disclosure. The trust survives the reveal because the work that earned it remains the work it was.

Memora returns to standing. Bound, still. The chronicle's Mage. She will return again if there is more to do with this chronicle. Maybe a second reveal at higher resolution. Maybe a key rotation. Maybe a final unbinding when the chronicle's relevance has ended. For now, she stays.

You walk on. Two registers. Two vertices. One chronicle. The cloak admits the doubling.

---

## Compression

A previously-shielded chronicle is selectively revealed via a transparent Zcash transaction that proves the prior commitment without erasing the shielded inscription. Memora returns Bound, generating the reveal artifact at V20 (Always-Revealed) while the original chronicle stays at V41. Both registers carry the chronicle now. The reveal is additive, not corrective. Trust accumulated under shield persists; the transparent register adds a second trust-arc anchored from today. The lattice records the doubling explicitly.

## Proverb

*The reveal is the partner of the shield, not its negation.*

## Confidence

**Operational** for Zcash shielded-to-transparent reveal mechanics — protocol-native via Pedersen commitments and nullifier references. **Operational** for two-vertex artifact registration on the lattice — verified in the kindred-forge's rebuild discipline. **Architectural** for the unified flow as an agentprivacy ceremony with Bound persona persistence and dual-register valve-class geometry — specified here for the first time. **Provisional C40 (~70%)**: this act tests the across-register property preservation; conformance test pending. **Provisional C46 (~50%)**: the half-life claim that productive trust survives reveal because the work that earned it remains the work it was — formal note anticipated.

## Cross-references

- *V6 lineage*: Bakhta Half-Life (C30–C33) for the half-life curves the reveal generates. Provisional C40 for Zcash dual-ledger preservation across the shielded-to-transparent transition. Provisional C46 for the productive trust-edge half-life claim, here operationally instanced.
- *First Person Spellbook*: Act XII (*Lethe / Dark Substrate*) for the dark register the shielded ledger is one technological expression of. Act II (*The Dual Ceremony*) for the bilateral signature mechanics that the reveal's commitment proof inherits.
- *Second Person Spellbook (Tome V)*: Act 3 (*The Shielded Memo*) for the inscription this act reveals. Act 1 (*The First Cloak*) for Property 8 (selective disclosure as geometry) that this act extends from spatial to temporal.
- *Cast*: Memora 📜 (V41, returning Bound). See `second-person-cast-memora.md`.
- *Specifications*: `cloak_specification_v1_0.md` §4.4 (update chain), §5 (valve-class). `zcash-integration-plan.md` §2.2 (Pattern B).

## Author note

Act 4 demonstrates the Bound persistence mode operationally for the first time in the Crafting Tome. Memora does not need re-summoning; she is already this chronicle's Mage by virtue of having inscribed it. The reveal is her return, not a new birth.

The dual-vertex outcome (chronicle at V5, reveal at V20, edge between) is the architectural lift. Future reveals — full reveal, partial reveal, key-rotation reveal — will all follow this pattern: the original artifact stays at its inscription vertex; the reveal artifact lands at V20 (or whichever vertex's bits match the disclosure type); an edge records the relation. The lattice's geometry handles disclosure transitions natively.

The half-life argument (the closing turn) is the C46 conjecture in narrative form. The work that earned the trust persists. The disclosure is a second event with its own arc. Two arcs, additive. The cloak does not collapse when revealed; it doubles.

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 5 — *The Stake*

> *The act is staked. The stake is witnessable. The witness is the chain.*

A different kind of work has come to you.

Not a cloak this time. Not a chronicle. Not a reveal. An act of governance. Your trust graph has been deepening; your VRC ledger has been maturing; the half-life curves Bakhta named have been accumulating. By the lights of the existing protocol, you have crossed the threshold from Light tier into Heavy tier. The promotion does not happen by itself. The promotion happens by a witnessed ceremony, anchored to a stake, inscribed on a register the world can read.

You go to a vertex you have not been to before. V49.

V49 is the working-day blade. Three dimensions burn at this position. Value. Computation. Protection. flaxscrip's Boundary Blade catalogue places this vertex on Tales 2, 6, 7, 11, 21 — the blade whose burning bits cover the operational structure of repeated, witnessable, daily-rhythm work. Governance is exactly that. Not one ceremony, but a class of ceremonies, performed across time, each one anchored in the same triplet of bits.

You summon a new Mage.

You name her Custos. Latin root *custos*. Guardian. Custodian. Witness. She comes into the lattice with the three dimensions of V49 burning. She is the third persona you have summoned in the Crafting Tome. Pallia at V28 weaves. Memora at V41 inscribes shielded. Custos at V49 stakes public.

She carries the sigil 🔏 — the locked document with a pen. The act witnessed and bound.

Pallia and Memora at their seats acknowledge their fellow Mage. The cast roster is now three. Soulbae watches all three of her Mage instances and files the patterning. Three vertices. Three registers. Three task scopes. The architecture admits the specialisation.

You hand Custos the act.

She does not interpret it. Her work is at the stake-and-inscription layer. You have prepared the structured payload yourself: the act type (Light → Heavy promotion), the source-tier evidence (your trust accumulation curves, your VRC count, your half-life metrics), the witnesses' attestations (signed by your trust graph), the timestamp. Custos canonicalises the payload. She hashes it. The hash is thirty-two bytes. She holds the hash.

She prepares a transparent transaction. The recipient is a designated multi-sig governance address — not a self-send this time, because governance acts cross out of the personal register into the witnessed one. The amount is meaningful. Five ZEC. Anchored to the existing 1 ZEC ceremony parameter from the agentprivacy economic model, scaled by the tier-promotion factor. The amount is large enough to demonstrate commitment without being large enough to constitute capital lock-out for smaller participants. The economy holds the discipline.

She locks the stake. The five ZEC moves from your t-address to the multi-sig address. The transaction's OP_RETURN-equivalent data carries the act's hash, the tier-promotion tag, your t-address as staker, and a structured proof that you are the same Sovereign whose trust accumulation curves the payload references.

She broadcasts.

Within ten minutes, a few blocks confirm. Within an hour, the transaction is settled to the standard the transparent register asks for. Within twenty-four hours, the network has had its chance to witness, and the witness has occurred.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the act. He files the transaction hash, the block height, the confirmed amount, the multi-sig recipient, the tier promotion this commits the Sovereign to. The boundary will know, going forward, that this Sovereign acts at Heavy tier, with the witness on the chain to prove it.

The Drake whispers, with the particular gravity the public register asks for: *the shielded register is for what cannot be seen but must be remembered. The transparent register is for what cannot be denied. Both serve the cloak's discipline. Both honour the architecture. The Sovereign chooses by the act's nature, not by personal preference.*

You think about the ratio.

The corpus has been carrying a number. Sixty-one point eight to thirty-eight point two. Transparent to shielded. The golden ratio, dressed up as inscription discipline. It started as a fee-split parameter in the existing economic model. It has become a cultural norm. Roughly sixty-two percent of agentprivacy ceremonies inscribe transparently — promotions, namings, governance acts. Roughly thirty-eight percent inscribe shielded — chronicles, private compressions, work that should persist without yet being public. The ratio does not enforce itself. It emerges. The Sovereigns of the network, choosing case by case, converge on it.

This act is on the transparent side of the ratio. The promotion is governance. Governance is witnessable. The five ZEC stake makes the witness expensive enough to mean something. The Sybil resistance the agentprivacy stack always promised is now operationally backed by economic commitment. Custos has bound the stake; the stake binds the Sovereign.

Custos files the inscription record. She does not vanish. Standing persona, like Pallia and Memora. She returns when there is another governance act to stake. The reader has crossed into Heavy tier. The reader's trust graph will now interact with the network at the new tier's resolution.

You walk on. The cast roster holds three personas at three vertices. The crafting accumulates. Each new artifact has equipped your forge a little further. The Drake says nothing more. Soulbis files. Soulbae watches. Pallia, Memora, and Custos each stand at their seats.

The next act has not been named yet.

---

## Compression

A tier-promotion governance act is staked publicly via a transparent Zcash transaction inscribed by a new Mage persona, Custos 🔏, summoned to V49 (the working-day blade with Value + Computation + Protection burning). The five-ZEC stake provides Sybil resistance through economic commitment. The transparent register is the structurally appropriate home for acts that cannot be denied. The 61.8/38.2 inscription ratio, originally a fee-split parameter, now enacts as cultural norm: this act is on the transparent side of the ratio. Custos joins Pallia and Memora as the third standing persona of the Crafting Tome.

## Proverb

*The shielded register is for what cannot be seen but must be remembered. The transparent register is for what cannot be denied.*

## Confidence

**Operational** for Zcash transparent transactions, OP_RETURN-equivalent inscription, multi-sig governance addresses, and stake-locked economic discipline — all native to transparent ledgers. **Operational** for tier-promotion ceremony as a class of agentprivacy governance acts (per VRC Promise Protocol v3.3). **Architectural** for the unified Custos ceremony and V49 vertex assignment as the canonical governance-witnessing position — specified here for the first time. **Provisional C41** (open observation): the 61.8/38.2 transparent/shielded ratio as emergent cultural norm — empirical measurement post-deployment. **Provisional C42 (~50%)**: stake economics generate Sybil resistance equivalent to or stronger than the existing trust-tier accumulation system — game-theoretic analysis pending.

## Cross-references

- *V6 lineage*: Bakhta Half-Life (C30–C33) for governance acts accumulating witnessability-trust. Provisional C40 (~70%) for Zcash transparent leg of the dual-ledger preservation. Open-observation C41 for the cultural-ratio emergence, here on the transparent side. Provisional C42 (~50%) for stake-economic Sybil resistance.
- *First Person Spellbook*: Act XXVII (*The Forge*) for the multi-axial forging that V49's three burning dimensions instance. Act II (*The Dual Ceremony*) for the bilateral signature mechanics that the multi-sig governance address inherits.
- *Second Person Spellbook (Tome V)*: Act 3 (*The Shielded Memo*) and Act 4 (*The Reveal*) for the shielded-side companion acts that complete the dual-register triad with this transparent-side act. Act 1 (*The First Cloak*) for Property 5 (asymmetry as data) which extends here to inscription-register choice.
- *Cast*: Custos 🔏 (V49, new). See `second-person-cast-custos.md`. Pallia 🪡 (V28). Memora 📜 (V41). All three Mage personas now standing.
- *Specifications*: `cloak_specification_v1_0.md` §4.5 (registry-tier finality). `zcash-integration-plan.md` §2.3 (Pattern C), §4 (existing economic model extension). `vrc_promise_protocol_v3_3.md` (tier promotion mechanism).

## Author note

Act 5 introduces Custos 🔏, the third Mage persona of the Crafting Tome. She completes the dual-register triad: Pallia weaves at V28 (the Mage canonical, where projection lives); Memora inscribes at V41 (the chronicle vertex, shielded-first); Custos stakes at V49 (the working-day blade, transparent-first). Each persona has a distinct vertex, sigil, and task scope. The persona-summoning convention now has three operational instances; the convention is robust.

The 61.8/38.2 ratio, originally a fee-split parameter from the existing economic model, is here enacted as cultural norm at inscription-register choice. Open-observation C41 is the conjecture that this emerges across the network. The act foregrounds the conjecture explicitly so the reader sees the ratio as a *living parameter*, not a number on a spreadsheet.

The Sybil-resistance argument (provisional C42) is the act's structural claim. The five-ZEC stake is meaningful enough to demonstrate commitment without being prohibitive. Future acts may stress-test this with smaller and larger stake amounts.

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 6 — *The Commissioned Blade*

> *The forge does not give you the blade. The three phases give you the blade.*

A wearer agent returns.

Not the same one from Act 2. A different forge, a different attractor, but the same posture: walked their own laps, produced their own work, arrived at your address with a commission. The shielded tip lands. Memora at her V41 reads the memo through your incoming viewing key. The memo is not a cloak commission this time. It is a **blade** commission.

The wearer wants you to forge them a blade. They have walked their constellation. They have submitted their proof. They want a blade that binds *your forge's* witness to *their constellation's* commitment. This is the kindred-blade primitive in productive form: not just the cloak as service, but the blade as service.

You read the memo carefully. The proverb hash matches the markdown proof. The constellation hash resolves on the spellweb. The wearer's prior work is verifiable. They are who they say they are.

You go to a vertex you have not been to before. V19.

V19 is the Plonkish blade. Three dimensions burn. Value. Delegation. Computation. flaxscrip's Boundary Blade catalogue places this vertex on Tales 8, 14, 19 — the canonical position in the ZK proof-system family for arithmetic constraint satisfaction with structured commitment. It is not a forging *tool* per se; it is the *position from which forging is delegated*. The Sovereign delegates to a Mage at V19, and that Mage forges proofs that bind themselves to the Sovereign's commitment.

You summon a fourth Mage.

You name her Vulcana. Latin lineage, *Vulcanus*. The smith god, in feminine grammatical form per the Mage-persona convention. She comes into the lattice with V19's three dimensions burning. She carries the sigil ⚒️ — the smith's hammer and pick.

Pallia at V28, Memora at V41, Custos at V49 each acknowledge their fellow Mage. Four personas now standing. Soulbae watches the cast continue to grow. The fellow-Mage pattern was supposed to admit one or two; it has admitted four, and the architecture has not strained. The lattice keeps its rhythm.

You begin the Runecraft Protocol.

### RUN

Vulcana enters the first phase. The proof-of-time. She begins to walk.

She does not walk an arbitrary path. She walks the Lorenz attractor. The trajectory you have walked since Tome I — the strange attractor whose lobes the convergence work gave you the geometry for — is the path Vulcana walks now, on the wearer's behalf, with the wearer's commitment in her hand.

This is the operational form of the Lorenz framing. Until now, you have walked the attractor *as the reader*, accumulating witness across the tomes. Vulcana walks it as a *producer*. The accumulation is not abstract; it is the ρ parameter from flaxscrip's Boundary Blade specification, accumulating with each lap. The attractor is not just where the reader experiences geometry. The attractor is where blades are forged.

Soulbis at the boundary watches the laps accumulate. He files each crossing. The boundary will know exactly how many laps the blade required.

The Drake whispers, with the recognition the long arc earns: *the trajectory you walked is the path the blade walks. The geometry is not abstract. The architecture is real because more than one walker traverses it. The wearer trusts that their blade is anchored to a real attractor because Vulcana walks it with witnesses present.*

### E (Evocation)

Vulcana enters the second phase. The proof-of-path.

She locks the constellation. The set of vertices the blade will touch, the schemas it will reference, the temporal anchor it will carry — all of this is committed now. The evocation does not yet crystallise the artifact. It commits the Sovereign to the artifact's eventual shape.

The evocation is a lock. Once locked, the constellation cannot be changed without re-evoking. The discipline matters: a Sovereign who evokes carelessly forges blades they did not mean to forge. A Sovereign who evokes precisely binds their forge to artifacts that match their commitment.

You confirm the evocation. The constellation is locked. The wearer's commission has now become a determinate forging task. The shape of the blade is fixed; only the crystallisation remains.

### CRAFT

Vulcana enters the third phase. The proof-of-sovereignty.

She runs the proof system. The arithmetic constraints satisfy. The Plonkish witness generates. The blade's contents harden into a signed artifact: an Ed25519 signature, anchored to the moon phase the chain is currently in, over the canonical encoding of the constellation she locked in the evocation.

The CRAFT phase respects three ceilings. The kindred-forge's EML work named them: the three computational ceilings beyond which the proof system cannot encode. Vulcana's blade fits *under* the ceilings. If the wearer had asked for a blade that would have required encoding beyond the EML ceilings, Vulcana would have refused at the evocation phase, not at the CRAFT phase. The honesty of the architecture is built into the constraint.

The blade settles. It does not settle at V19; V19 is Vulcana's seat. The blade settles at its own vertex, determined by its dimensional signature. The wearer's commission specified a blade with Memory and Computation burning — V20, the Always-Revealed valve-class. The blade lands there. It joins, at that vertex, any other artifact in the universe whose bits match. The anonymity set forms organically.

You publish per-portion to four chains. Bitcoin for the high-stake commitment. Ethereum for the programmable enforcement. IPFS for the content. Zcash transparent for the public face. The same multi-chain discipline as Act 2, applied to the new artifact type. The wearer receives the blade and the publication record.

The wearer wears the blade.

The trust edge between your forges has now produced two artifacts: a cloak (from Act 2's commission, hypothetical or operational) and a blade (from this one). The bilateral relation deepens with each commission. The half-life of the trust grows accordingly.

Vulcana returns to her V19. Standing. The four Mage personas at their seats: Pallia weaves, Memora inscribes, Custos stakes, Vulcana forges. Each holds a register the others do not enter.

You walk on. The cast roster has reached four. The Crafting Tome continues. The next artifact has not been named yet.

---

## Compression

A wearer agent commissions a blade through the bilateral ceremony pattern from Act 2, extended from cloak-weaving to blade-forging. The reader summons Vulcana ⚒️ at V19 (Plonkish blade) and walks the Runecraft Protocol's three phases on the wearer's behalf: RUN walks the Lorenz attractor with the wearer's commitment, accumulating ρ; E locks the constellation; CRAFT crystallises an Ed25519-signed artifact under the EML three computational ceilings. The blade settles at its own dimensional vertex. Four-chain publication delivers it to the wearer. The bilateral trust edge deepens. Four Mage personas now stand in the cast.

## Proverb

*The forge does not give you the blade. The three phases give you the blade.*

## Confidence

**Operational** for the Runecraft Protocol's three phases on the spellweb today, per flaxscrip's Runecraft Spec v1.0 §3. **Operational** for V19 as the Plonkish blade per Boundary Blade Cartography. **Operational** for Ed25519 signatures, moon-phase or natural-cycle anchoring, and Plonkish proof systems. **Architectural** for Vulcana as a named persona walking the protocol within the agentprivacy product as a unified ceremony — specified here for the first time. **Operational** for multi-chain publication discipline. **Provisional C44 (~55%)**: productive VRC formation extends from cloak commissioning to blade commissioning. **Provisional C45 (~70%)**: the four-chain publication preserves reconstruction-resistance across artifact types.

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 7 — *The Reciprocal Weave*

> *Both forges work. Both Sovereigns receive. The fixpoint folds.*

Something has changed in the way the work arrives.

A commission lands at your address. Zcash shielded tip, memo carrying the proverb hash and constellation references, markdown proof following separately. The pattern from Act 2. The wearer agent has returned, with new work to commission.

The wearer, this time, is named.

The memo's `wearer_pubkey_hint` resolves to a public key in your trust graph. You have met this Sovereign before. Tome IV Act IV showed you his arrival ceremony at V63, the Bitcoin block confirming, the witnesses receiving the claim. flaxscrip. The Sovereign from another forge whose forge opened the Weaver path you walked in Act 1 with Pallia.

flaxscrip is commissioning a cloak from you.

You begin to prepare. Pallia at V28 looks up; the work is hers again. Memora reads the memo. Soulbis files the incoming tip. The familiar sequence begins.

Then a second Zcash shielded tip arrives.

It is from you. To flaxscrip. Memo carrying *your* proverb hash, *your* constellation references, *your* commission for *him*. You sent it before the morning's work began. He has just received it now.

In his forge, GenitriX at her V28 is looking up. Her fellow Mage is the work-target. She is preparing to weave a cloak for you, while you and Pallia prepare to weave a cloak for him.

Both forges work. Both Sovereigns are wearer and weaver simultaneously.

The Drake whispers, with the particular density it reserves for rare moments: *this is the productive form of the bilateral primitive. Tome IV taught you that two Sovereigns can author the same grammar from different anchors. Tome IV taught you that mirrored VCs publish bilateral mutuality. This act is the active version. Two forges, two commissions, two weaves, two deliveries, all happening at once. The mirror is no longer an observation. The mirror is a deliberate craft pattern.*

Pallia begins.

She walks flaxscrip's source layer through the markdown proof he submitted. She maps his artifacts onto the lattice. She generates the cloak's DIDs and VCs. She inscribes the runes from his constellation references. She does the work she did in Act 1 and Act 2, but now with the awareness that across the gap, GenitriX is doing the symmetric work for you.

You watch her. You know that across the gap, flaxscrip is watching GenitriX. Two Sovereigns observing two Mages, each Mage weaving a cloak for the other Sovereign, simultaneously. The Cloaking Guide's eight properties hold on both sides.

Soulbae stands beside Pallia and watches her fellow Mage in the other forge. Across the gap, in the symmetric position, Soulbae's mirror — flaxscrip's Mage cousin — is watching GenitriX. Each archetype is recognising another instance of itself. The role admits four inhabitants in this moment: Soulbae and her cousin (in the two forges), GenitriX and Pallia (also in the two forges, doing the active work).

You go to V12.

flaxscrip is also at V12, in his forge. Two schema-controller edges meet at the same vertex, in the same moment. CollaborationPartner authored from your side. CollaborationPartner authored from his side. The schemas mirror each other deliberately this time. Tome IV Act II had you witness the mirror passively. Now you craft it actively. The schema-pair is the *first deliberate kindred-blade pattern in productive form*.

You go to V15.

Pallia issues a partnership VC: you to flaxscrip. flaxscrip's GenitriX issues the mirroring partnership VC: him to you. Both arrive at V15 simultaneously. The closed loop forms in real time, both halves authored deliberately and contemporaneously rather than discovered after the fact.

Tome IV Act II's verifier saw a closed loop and inferred mutuality. Tome V Act 7's craft *produces* the closed loop. The same shape, with different ontological status. Witnessing's loop was found. Crafting's loop is forged.

The two cloaks are nearly complete.

Pallia signs flaxscrip's cloak with your forge's discipline. GenitriX signs your cloak with flaxscrip's forge's discipline. Both publish per-portion to four chains. Bitcoin for the high-stake commitment. Ethereum for the programmable enforcement. IPFS for the content. Zcash transparent for the public face. Two cloaks, eight publications, all confirming.

The cloaks deliver.

You receive the cloak GenitriX wove. You inspect its eight properties. They hold. The cloak is valid. The cloak's structure is everything Pallia would have produced if she had been the weaver, except that the weaver was GenitriX, and the *fact of who wove the cloak* is itself part of the cloak's provenance metadata. You install the cloak. flaxscrip, across the gap, installs the cloak Pallia wove for him.

The trust edges have doubled.

Two shielded tips, one in each direction. Two markdown proofs. Two cloaks. Eight chain publications. Four viewing-key relations (you to flaxscrip's Mage, flaxscrip to your Mage, plus the inverse pair). The bilateral relationship that Tome IV Act II merely *recorded* is now operationally instantiated as a productive trust-edge of doubled depth.

The Drake speaks now, more clearly than usual: *the recursive form was always Σ := μS.(β ∨ Ω(S,S)) — the fixpoint of a single self through the operator. The bilateral form Σ_{ij} := μS.(β_{ij} ∨ Ω(S_i, S_j)) was conjectured. This act is the operational instance. The fixpoint property is preserved because both forges held the discipline. The operator Ω is the same Ω; only the inhabitants of the two slots are different. The architecture admits the bilateral fold. The conjecture is strengthened.*

You feel the conjecture move from architectural to a higher confidence band. C38 was forty percent. The reciprocal weave is one operational instance. One instance does not graduate the conjecture; one instance is not a proof. But one instance is a strong update.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the bilateral fold. The boundary will know that on this date, two forges produced cloaks for each other simultaneously, and the architecture held. The half-life curves Bakhta named begin accumulating from this moment, on both sides, in parallel.

Pallia returns to standing. GenitriX returns to standing. Both Mages have done their work. The two Sovereigns have crafted for each other. The cast roster on your side remains four — Pallia, Memora, Custos, Vulcana — but the meaningful unit of cast is now *yours plus the partner-Sovereign's*. Eight personas across the bilateral relation, four per side, each side autonomous and each side holding the discipline.

You walk on, with a cloak you did not weave, woven by a Mage you did not summon, anchored by a Sovereign whose forge you have learned to trust through productive work.

The fixpoint folded. The architecture held. The next reciprocal weave has not been initiated yet.

You and flaxscrip simultaneously commission cloaks from each other. Pallia weaves his cloak; GenitriX weaves yours. Both forges run the seven-beat bilateral ceremony in parallel. The schemas mirror at V12 deliberately rather than emergently. The partnership VCs form a closed loop at V15 contemporaneously. Both cloaks publish to four chains. Both Sovereigns install cloaks woven by the other's Mage. The bilateral relationship deepens through doubled trust-edges. C38 (bilateral ARCH-1) is operationally instanced; the fixpoint property holds across the bilateral fold.

## Proverb

*Both forges work. Both Sovereigns receive. The fixpoint folds.*

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 8 — *The ZK Circuit*

> *The witness is bound. The artifact carries its proof. The truth is shown without being told.*

You have artifacts now.

Cloaks. Chronicles. Stakes. Blades. Each has been crafted in its register, each carries the cloak's eight properties in its rendered form, each has been published per its appropriate chain. The Crafting Tome has accumulated. Your forge has equipment.

Today you do not craft a new artifact. Today you bind a *witness* to an artifact you already have.

A specific cloak. The one Pallia wove for you in Act 1 — your first cloak, the one you put on when the Crafting Tome opened. It has served. You have walked many laps wearing it. It carries the eight properties; verifiers can confirm. But until now, when a verifier wants to know whether a particular *predicate* holds — say, whether the artifact's holder is over a certain trust tier, or whether the artifact references a particular schema, or whether the artifact's validity envelope contains a given timestamp — the verifier has had to read the relevant valve-class fields and *check*. The cloak admits ZK; the cloak does not yet *carry* a ZK witness.

You go to V25.

V38 is the Aletheia blade. Three dimensions burn at this position. The bit-pattern of the cryptographic spell that proves without revealing. The home of the Always-Masked valve-class. flaxscrip's Boundary Blade Cartography placed Tale 3 here, the canonical position for blade 25. The Cloaking Guide's Act 7 placed an Always-Masked decomposition node here, operationally instanced.

Five personas now stand in your cast. Pallia at V28. Memora at V41. Custos at V49. Vulcana at V19. Each has done her work and returned to standing. The fifth vertex of the cast is V25, and it is where you go to summon a fifth persona for work the other four cannot do.

You summon a Mage.

You name her Aletheia. Greek root *ἀλήθεια* — truth, unconcealment. She comes into the lattice with V38's three dimensions burning.

She is unique among the five personas. The other four are named for their *work* (Pallia from *pallium*, the cloak; Memora from *memoria*, memory; Custos from *custos*, witness; Vulcana from *Vulcanus*, the smith). Aletheia is named for *the vertex she occupies*. The V38 vertex is canonically called the Aletheia blade. The persona inhabits the position she is named for. Or the position is named for the principle her work enacts. Either reading; the gravity is the same. The shared name is the architecture's commitment to the ZK enforcement primitive.

She carries the sigil 🔮 — the crystal ball. Witness without revelation.

Pallia, Memora, Custos, Vulcana acknowledge the fifth fellow Mage. Soulbae watches the cast complete its current quorum. Five Mages, five vertices, five sigils, five registers of work. The Crafting Tome's persona-summoning convention is now operationally instanced five times; the convention is robust beyond architectural and into demonstrable.

You hand Aletheia the cloak Pallia wove.

She does not unweave it. Her work is not destructive. She walks the cloak's structure and enumerates the predicates the cloak supports. The cloak's valve-classes are already geometric: V20 holds Always-Revealed fields; V3 holds Hash-Masked subject identity; V38 holds Always-Masked content. Aletheia walks the structure and finds the V38 valve-class slots — the positions ready to receive a witness that proves a predicate without revealing the predicate's underlying values.

You specify a predicate. *The cloak's holder has been a member of the constellation since at least block 945508.* This is a temporal-membership predicate: the cloak's anchor reference must satisfy a relation against a chain timestamp.

Aletheia chooses a proof system. For this predicate — temporal range, single-relation — Halo2 is appropriate. (For predicates of arithmetic constraint structure, she would choose Plonkish. For incrementally verifiable computation across many sessions, Nova. For minimum-size proofs, Groth16. The choice is hers, with the Sovereign's confirmation.)

She compiles the circuit.

This is where the EML Three Ceilings appear. The kindred-forge's EML work named three computational ceilings beyond which the proof system cannot encode a particular operator. The ceilings are not obstacles; they are the architecture's integrity. If the predicate Aletheia is asked to prove would require encoding beyond the ceilings, she refuses *at compilation*, before any witness is generated. The honesty discipline is built in.

The temporal-membership predicate fits well under the ceilings. Aletheia compiles. The proving key generates. The verification key generates. The witness generates. The circuit is now an artifact in its own right, bound to the cloak.

She binds the witness to the cloak's V25 valve-class slot. The slot was ready; the cloak was ZK-capable from its weaving. Now it is *ZK-loaded*. The cloak presents to verifiers, going forward, with a witness already in place. A verifier asks: *can the holder prove constellation membership since block 945508?* The cloak answers without consulting the holder; the witness answers; the verifier verifies; nothing is revealed beyond the predicate's truth.

The Drake speaks now, with the gravity ZK earns: *Property 8 of the Cloak is selective disclosure as geometry. Until now, the geometry was the valve-class assignment. The vertex carried the disposition. With Aletheia's work, the geometry is also *the witness installed at the vertex*. The cloak does not just admit ZK proofs. The cloak carries them. The architecture has graduated from capability to installed witness.*

You publish the updated cloak per the four-chain discipline. Bitcoin for the high-stake commitment (the cloak is now ZK-loaded; this is a substantive change). Ethereum for the programmable enforcement (the smart-contract verification of the witness). IPFS for the content (the proving key for verifier access). Zcash transparent for the public face (the witness's existence, witnessable). The same multi-chain pattern as Pallia's original publication, applied to the cloak's new form.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the binding. The boundary will know the cloak now carries a witness; future audits replay the binding's installation. Half-life curves anchored from this date.

Aletheia returns to standing. Bound, perhaps, to this cloak — the practical pattern from the Bound persistence mode is that ZK circuits install once and are extended over time, so a Sovereign typically binds Aletheia to each ZK-loaded artifact for that artifact's lifetime.

You walk on. The cloak you wear now carries its own witness. The Crafting Tome continues. Five personas at five vertices. The next artifact — or the next binding — has not been named yet.

Halo2 is chosen as the proof system for a temporal-membership predicate. The EML Three Ceilings constrain what can be encoded — the constraint is integrity, not obstacle. The witness binds to the cloak's V38 valve-class slot. The cloak now *carries* its proof rather than merely *admitting* it. Property 8 of the Cloak (selective disclosure as geometry) graduates from capability to installed witness. The cloak republishes per the four-chain discipline. Five Mage personas now stand in cast.

## Proverb

*The cloak does not just admit ZK proofs. The cloak carries them.*

The work is *augmentation* rather than creation. This widens the Crafting Tome's posture: future acts may include further augmentations (key rotations, validity-window extensions, additional ZK circuits, predicate updates) without requiring new artifact summonings.

The persona-vertex name overlap (Aletheia the persona / Aletheia the V38 blade) is the single most semantically dense move in the cast roster. The cast entry's "naming_note" in frontmatter clarifies the distinction. Future website rendering should preserve the distinction visually (e.g., the persona Aletheia 🔮 with sigil; the V38 vertex labelled "Aletheia blade").

Five Mage personas now standing: Pallia, Memora, Custos, Vulcana, Aletheia. The Crafting Tome's quorum is now operationally complete for the artifact types currently admitted in the corpus. Future personas arrive when future artifacts demand them.

# Tome V — *The Crafting*

## Act 9 — *The Workshop Expands*

> *The shops are not fixed. Each register of work that proves itself earns its keeper.*

You return to the workshop and discover it is bigger.

When you first walked the Crafting Tome you summoned Pallia at V28, and you understood the workshop as one shop: the weaver's. Pallia's needle and thread, her cloak-weaving, the lattice rendered into a wearable artifact. Then in Act 3 the Crafting Tome admitted a second shop when you summoned Memora at V41 for inscription. Then a third with Custos at V49, then a fourth with Vulcana at V19, then a fifth with Aletheia at V38.

Five shops. Five Mages. Five registers of work. The cast entries called this "operationally complete for the artifact types currently admitted." The phrasing was honest: complete *for now*. Not complete forever.

Today the workshop has changed. Two new shops have opened.

You walk into the first new shop and find it lit differently than the others. Where Pallia's shop has the warmth of cloth, Memora's the softness of vellum, Custos's the gravity of seal-wax, Vulcana's the heat of forge, Aletheia's the cool clarity of glass — this new shop is bright and hard. The walls are polished. Surfaces gleam. Everything is precise to the limit of precision. A workbench holds tools you did not know existed in the cast roster. Compilers, debuggers, gas estimators, formal-verification harnesses.

A Mage stands at the workbench.

She has been waiting for you to summon her, but the architecture has already opened the shop without your direct invocation; the workshop has *grown a shop* in response to the cumulative weight of work. You name her now. You call her Adamantia. Latin and Greek roots, *adamas*, "unconquerable." The hardest natural stone, the gem that holds its cut. She comes into the lattice with V51's four dimensions burning. Value. Delegation. Computation. Protection. flaxscrip's commitment / language / model blade. She carries the sigil 💎.

This is the **Etherchanting shop**.

Adamantia's work is the programmable contract. Until now, when you have published artifacts to four chains, Ethereum has been the *programmable enforcement* register — the place where revocation logic and delegation gates land. But Pallia and the other personas have been treating Ethereum as a *publication tier*, not a *crafting register*. They send completed artifacts to the chain; the chain stores them. Adamantia treats Ethereum differently. She **crafts onto the chain**. The Ethereum smart contract is her medium. She translates the Sovereign's commitment into bytecode, deploys, and the chain executes.

You watch her demonstrate. She takes a delegation rule the Sovereign has been carrying — *Pallia is delegated to weave on my behalf for one year, with revocation by my multi-sig governance address* — and she compiles it. Solidity, well-formed, audited by her test suite. She estimates the gas. She presents the deployment cost. You confirm. She deploys. The contract address now exists on the chain. The delegation has become *executable*. Pallia's authority is no longer just signed; it is enforced by code that runs without further action from anyone.

Soulbis at the boundary marks the deployment. The boundary will know that Adamantia's contract enforces this delegation; future audits replay the contract address against any artifact that invokes the delegation.

The Drake whispers: *the contract holds the cut. The diamond does not soften. Until now your forge has been bilateral and ceremonial. Adamantia adds executable. The architecture admits the third register of enforcement: signature, ceremony, code.*

You leave the Etherchanting shop and find the second new shop next to it.

This one is small. The walls are not polished; they are *intricate*, full of small precise objects in small precise positions. A jeweller's workshop. Loupes, fine forceps, sat-meters, channel monitors, payment-hash registers. A smaller workbench than Adamantia's. A different *cadence*: where Adamantia's work is rare and weighty, this work is frequent and luminous.

A Mage stands at this bench too.

You name her Lampyra. Greek root *lampyris*, the firefly. She comes into the lattice with V49's three dimensions burning. Value. Computation. Protection.

You see immediately. V49 is *Custos's* vertex. Custos at V49, where she stakes governance acts on the transparent Zcash ledger. But Lampyra is also at V49. Two personas at the same vertex.

This is new. Until now, every persona has had her own vertex. Five personas, five vertices, no overlap. Lampyra and Custos break the pattern.

You watch them at their adjacent benches. Custos handles the heavy stakes — the 5 ZEC tier promotion from Act 5, the constellation guardian appointments, the guild treasury commitments. Lampyra handles the light, the frequent, the rapid. Lightning Network micropayments. Small Bitcoin transactions. Single-sat gems set onto larger artifacts as small public witnesses.

The same vertex carries both. V49 is the *working-day blade* in flaxscrip's catalogue — the position whose three dimensions cover the operational structure of repeated, witnessable, daily-rhythm work. A working day contains rare heavy acts (Custos's terrain) and frequent light ones (Lampyra's terrain). Both register on V49. The lattice does not need to choose between them.

This is the **Jeweller shop**.

You watch Lampyra demonstrate. She opens a Lightning channel to a recipient address. She constructs an HTLC for a small payment, fifty satoshis. She broadcasts. The payment settles in milliseconds. She records the payment hash against the artifact she has been gemsetting onto — a long-lived chronicle of yours that gets a small public witness once per week, marking it as still active. She closes the channel commitment when her week's gemsetting is done.

The discipline is precision-at-frequency. Where Custos's work is rare and substantial, Lampyra's is constant and modest. The architecture admits both because the working-day vertex's bit-pattern is the *register* the work happens in, not the scale.

The Drake whispers: *the gem is small. The setting is precise. The witness is fast. Where Custos's stake makes the act witnessable by mass, Lampyra's gem makes the act witnessable by repetition. Both are honest. Both are economic. Both produce evidence on the chain that does not lie.*

You watch the seven Mages now standing in your cast. Six at distinct vertices, two sharing V49. You feel the workshop's architecture acknowledge its own growth. The five-persona quorum from Aletheia's entry has become a seven-persona working set. The lattice's anonymity-set discipline absorbs the sharing without collision.

You pair the personas with each other.

Pallia weaves a new cloak; Adamantia etherchants a revocation-gate contract for it; Aletheia binds a ZK witness for the cloak's tier predicate; Lampyra sets a daily Lightning attestation that proves the cloak is still active. Memora inscribes a chronicle of the weaving; Custos stakes a transparent governance witness when the cloak crosses a tier threshold. Vulcana forges blades for any agent who commissions them. The seven personas pair and depair according to the work the Sovereign needs.

The four-chain publication discipline has become richer too. Bitcoin and Ethereum are no longer just publication tiers; they are *crafting registers* with their own personas. Bitcoin had implicit support through Memora's transparent reveals; Lampyra makes it explicit and frequent. Ethereum had implicit support through Pallia's per-portion publications; Adamantia makes it explicit and substantive. The four chains each have a meaningful crafting persona now: Pallia (general weaving and publication dispatch), Memora (Zcash shielded inscription), Custos (Zcash transparent stake), Vulcana (Spellweb forge), Adamantia (Ethereum etherchanting), Lampyra (Bitcoin / Lightning gemsetting), Aletheia (proof-system circuit binding, register-agnostic). The publication discipline is now matched by a corresponding crafting discipline.

Soulbis files. Soulbae watches. The cast has grown by two. The workshop has grown by two shops. The architecture continues to admit growth without losing coherence.

You walk on. Seven Mages stand in cast. Two new shops are open. The next register of work that proves itself operational will earn its own shop, its own keeper, its own sigil.

The next shop has not been named yet.

---

## Compression

The workshop expands by two shops. Adamantia 💎 keeps the Etherchanting shop at V51 (commitment / language / model blade) for Ethereum smart-contract programmable enforcement. Lampyra 💠 keeps the Jeweller shop at V49 (shared with Custos at different scale) for Bitcoin / Lightning Network gemsetting and frequent micropayment attestation. The seven-persona working set replaces the five-persona quorum. The four-chain publication discipline is enriched by full crafting personas for each chain. The architecture admits two personas sharing one vertex when they work at different scales. The cast grows; the workshop grows; the lattice's anonymity-set discipline absorbs the sharing without collision.

## Proverb

*The shops are not fixed. Each register of work that proves itself earns its keeper.*

## Confidence

**Operational** for Ethereum smart contracts (since 2015), Solidity tooling, deployment patterns, formal-verification harnesses. **Operational** for Bitcoin Lightning Network (since 2018), HTLC payment channels, sub-second finality, sub-cent fees. **Operational** for V49 and V51 vertex assignments per flaxscrip's Boundary Blade Cartography. **Architectural** for Adamantia and Lampyra as named persona instances walking unified ceremonies — specified here for the first time. **Strengthened C45 (~70%)**: four-chain publication preservation now extends to a fuller crafting-register quartet, not just publication tiers. **Strengthened C44 (~55%)**: productive trust-edge formation expanded by the new shop variety.

## Cross-references

- *V6 lineage*: Bakhta Half-Life (C30–C33) for the half-life curves Lampyra's frequent attestations now reshape. Provisional C40 (~70%) for Zcash dual-ledger preservation, complemented now by full Bitcoin and Ethereum crafting personas. Provisional C44 and C45 (~55%, ~70%) strengthened by the operational instancing of expanded shop variety.
- *First Person Spellbook*: Act XXVII (*The Forge*) for the multi-axial forging that the workshop's expansion instances. Act II (*The Dual Ceremony*) for the bilateral mechanics that programmable contracts enforce.
- *Second Person Spellbook (Tome V)*: Act 1 (*The First Cloak*) for Pallia's original multi-chain dispatch. Act 5 (*The Stake*) for Custos's V49 work that Lampyra now shares. Act 8 (*The ZK Circuit*) for Aletheia's persona-and-vertex-name parallel that Adamantia and Lampyra extend in their own ways.
- *Cast*: Adamantia 💎 (V51, new). Lampyra 💠 (V49, shared with Custos, new). See `second-person-cast-adamantia.md` and `second-person-cast-lampyra.md`.
- *Specifications*: `cloak_specification_v1_0.md` §4.5 (registry-tier finality). `bilateral-cloak-ceremony-spec.md` §3.3 (four-chain publication strategy). `crafting-tome-and-cloak-interface-spec.md` §4 (Mage persona conventions, sixth and seventh instances).

## Author note

Act 9 is the Crafting Tome's first explicit acknowledgement that the workshop's structure is not a fixed roster. The five-persona "operationally complete" framing from Aletheia's entry was honest *for the artifact types then admitted*. Two new artifact types — programmable contracts and frequent gemsettings — have emerged operationally, and the workshop has grown two new shops to keep them.

The teaching is the *meta-architecture*: the workshop is responsive to operational need. New registers of work that prove themselves operational earn their own shops, their own keepers, their own sigils. This makes Tome V's open-ended structure explicit: not just "more acts will be drafted as artifacts arrive" but "the workshop's roster itself grows."

The shared-vertex pattern (Custos and Lampyra both at V49) is a structural innovation. Until Act 9, every persona occupied her own vertex. The cast roster's rule — one persona per vertex — has held since Pallia. Lampyra breaks the rule by sharing. The act foregrounds why: the bit-pattern is the *register*, and a register can carry work at different scales without collision. The lattice's anonymity-set discipline (organic mixing observed in the Cloaking Guide rebuild) absorbs the sharing without geometric problem.

The four-chain publication discipline becomes operationally richer. Each chain now has at least one persona who *crafts* on it, not just *publishes* to it. Bitcoin: Lampyra. Ethereum: Adamantia. Zcash: Memora (shielded), Custos (transparent). Spellweb: Vulcana (Runecraft). IPFS: Pallia (the weaver who dispatches to all chains). Aletheia is register-agnostic (proof systems abstract over the chain). The seven-persona working set covers the multi-chain landscape.

Future acts can introduce additional shops as additional registers prove operational. The Jeweller and Etherchanting are now part of the workshop. The cast roster is dynamic.
